# Turn the feedback step into a real AI-rated phase

## Goal

The `--with-feedback` flag (already shipped in commit `e7555135`) currently dispatches a mechanical scaffolder that writes an empty `feedback.md` and transitions to DONE. Replace that with a real worker phase: a model (Claude `:low` by default) reads digests of every artifact in the plan dir, rates each workflow stage 0-10 with a comment, and writes a populated `feedback.md`. User can still edit afterward — AI ratings live in parallel `ai_*` fields so user edits are distinguishable from model output.

## Why

`feedback.md` is supposed to be the corpus that flows back into rubric/profile tuning (the `--min-rating` / `--max-rating` search filters in `feedback.py` make this explicit). Empty templates that the user has to manually rate after every run never get filled in. An auto-rated draft that the user reviews/overrides is the difference between "feature shipped" and "useful data corpus."

## What's already in place (don't reinvent)

- **State machine** — `STATE_EXECUTED → review → STATE_REVIEWED → feedback → STATE_DONE`. Done.
- **`--with-feedback` flag wiring** — `megaplan/cli.py` (init parser), `megaplan/handlers/init.py` (persistence), `megaplan/_core/workflow.py` (`_with_feedback_from_state`, workflow patching). Done.
- **Existing handler dispatch** — `handle_feedback` at `megaplan/cli.py:1324` has a `workflow` operation branch (`cli.py:1386-1412`) that's currently the no-op scaffolder. Replace its body.
- **Auto driver** — `megaplan/auto.py` already routes `feedback` as a phase via `_phase_command`. Done.
- **Schema** — `megaplan/feedback.py`: `STAGES`, `_STAGE_BLURBS`, `StageFeedback(rating, comment)`, `PlanFeedback(overall, stages)`, `render_template`, `parse_feedback`, `load_feedback`. Extend, don't replace.
- **44 existing tests** in `tests/test_with_feedback.py`. Most stay valid; the workflow-mode handler test that asserts "no subprocess.run" gets revised (now it DOES call a worker — but never opens `$EDITOR`).

## What to build

### 1. Extend `feedback.py` schema with AI provenance fields

`megaplan/feedback.py`:

- Add `ai_rating: int | None` and `ai_comment: str | None` fields to `StageFeedback`. Default both `None`.
- `is_empty` should still return True when ALL four fields are unset.
- `to_dict` emits all four (`rating`, `comment`, `ai_rating`, `ai_comment`).
- Extend `render_template` to accept an optional `prefilled: PlanFeedback | None` arg. When provided, write `ai_rating:` / `ai_comment:` for each stage from the prefilled data. The user-editable `rating:` / `comment:` lines stay blank.
- Extend `parse_feedback` to parse both pairs of fields. Each section now recognizes `ai_rating:` / `ai_comment:` AND `rating:` / `comment:`.
- Add `effective_rating(stage_feedback) -> int | None` helper: returns `rating` if set, else `ai_rating`. Used by search filters so `--min-rating 7` matches AI-rated runs the user hasn't touched yet.
- Update `_filter_feedback_rows` in `cli.py:1240` to use `effective_rating` instead of hardcoded `rating` lookup.

### 2. New prompt module — `megaplan/prompts/feedback.py`

Build the prompt from per-stage artifact digests. The handler passes in the plan_dir + state; the prompt module reads what's there and assembles digests.

Structure:

```
build_feedback_prompt(plan_dir, state) -> str
```

Each digest is 2-5 sentences summarizing what the phase produced:
- **prep** (if `prep.json` exists): research scope + key findings
- **plan** (`plan_v*.md`): final plan summary, iteration count
- **critique** (`critique_output.json` / `critique_v*.json`): flag count, top issue categories
- **revise** (plan diffs across versions): what changed v1→vN
- **gate** (`gate.json`): recommendation + passed
- **finalize** (`final.md`): task count, batches
- **execute** (`execution.json`, `execution_audit.json`, `execution_batch_*.json`): tasks done/skipped/blocked, batch count, files changed
- **review** (`review.json`): verdict, summary
- **Run meta** (always): robustness, profile, iteration count, total cost from `state["meta"]["total_cost_usd"]`, history durations per phase

Stages that didn't run (because of robustness) are listed as "did not run" and not requested for rating.

The prompt itself:

```
You are a retrospective evaluator. Your job is to rate the quality of each
phase of a completed megaplan run.

This rating feeds into rubric tuning. Be honest. Errors of leniency cost
more than errors of severity — if a phase produced mediocre output that
later phases worked around, mark it ~5, not ~8.

Rate quality only, not cost-effectiveness. A great run that burned $50 is
still a 9 if the output is excellent. A cheap run with sloppy output is
not a 9 because it was cheap.

Scale (0-10):
- 10: textbook; no notes.
- 8: solid; minor polish only.
- 6: workable but with real issues — wasted iterations, missed flags,
     over/under-engineering.
- 4: degraded; the phase didn't do its job, downstream had to compensate.
- 2: actively harmful; produced output that hurt later stages.
- 0: complete failure.

Per-stage rubric:
- prep: did research surface useful info, or was it filler?
- plan: did the plan structure the work appropriately?
- critique: did it catch real issues, or stamp / over-flag?
- revise: did revise actually address critique flags?
- gate: was the decision well-calibrated?
- finalize: did the final plan land cleanly?
- execute: did the executor follow the plan? Add/remove unnecessary scope?
- review: did review catch real issues, or rubber-stamp?

Overall: weighted impression of run quality.

Each comment must be one sentence — what specifically drove the rating.

[digests follow]

Respond with strict JSON only:
{"overall": {"rating": int, "comment": str},
 "stages": {"<stage>": {"rating": int, "comment": str}, ...}}

Only include stages that actually ran.
```

### 3. Handler — `handle_feedback` workflow branch

Replace the body at `megaplan/cli.py:1386-1412`. New flow:

1. Verify `current_state == STATE_REVIEWED`. (Already done; keep.)
2. If `feedback.md` exists AND any user fields (`rating:` / `comment:`) are populated AND `--force` is NOT set: skip the AI pass entirely. Just transition to DONE. (Respects manual override.)
3. Otherwise: build prompt via `build_feedback_prompt(plan_dir, state)`, dispatch through the standard worker path used by other prompt-based phases (critique/review are the references). The phase model resolves through the same profile/slot mechanism — see (5).
4. Parse the model's JSON response. On parse failure or schema violation: log a warning, write a feedback.md with EMPTY `ai_*` fields (so user can still rate manually), transition to DONE. Don't fail the phase — feedback failure must never sink an otherwise-done plan.
5. Render `feedback.md` via the extended `render_template(name, idea=..., prefilled=parsed_feedback)`.
6. Atomic write. Transition state to DONE.
7. Return `StepResponse` with `feedback_path`, `feedback_present: True`, `ai_filled: bool`, `state: "done"`.

`--force` flag: add to the `feedback` subparser. Means "regenerate `ai_*` fields even if `feedback.md` already exists; never touch user `rating:`/`comment:` fields." When --force is set on a `feedback.md` that has user fields, preserve them — only overwrite `ai_*`.

### 4. Worker dispatch

Look at how `handle_critique` or `handle_review` dispatch their worker calls. Same pattern: resolve phase model from profile, build worker, call, get text back, parse. The new phase name is `feedback`. Use the existing infrastructure — `megaplan/workers.py` and friends.

### 5. Profile slots

Every profile in `megaplan/profiles/` gets a `feedback` key. Default model: Claude at `:low` across every tier. Exact specs:

- `basic.toml`: `feedback = "claude:low"`
- `led.toml`: `feedback = "claude:low"`
- `thoughtful.toml`: `feedback = "claude:low"`
- `premium.toml` (claude variant): `feedback = "claude:low"`
- `premium.toml` (codex variant): `feedback = "claude:low"` (intentional — feedback is fixed-vendor for cross-run comparability)
- `super-premium.toml` / `poirot.toml` / `standard.toml` (vendor-locked): `feedback = "claude:low"`
- `all-claude.toml`: `feedback = "claude"` (matches the no-suffix convention)
- `all-codex.toml`: `feedback = "claude:low"` (cross-vendor — feedback is independent of run vendor)
- Detective-cluster legacy profiles (`marlowe-*`, `spade-*`, `holmes-*`, `watson-*`, `nancy`): `feedback = "claude:low"`
- All-deepseek profiles, `all-open`: `feedback = "claude:low"` (only premium phase in an otherwise-open run; the cost is acceptable because feedback runs once per `--with-feedback` plan)

**`--vendor` flag does NOT affect feedback** — feedback stays Claude regardless of which premium vendor is doing plan/critique/etc. This is the cross-comparability principle: a `thoughtful @claude` run and a `thoughtful @codex` run should be rated by the same evaluator.

**`--critic` flag does NOT extend to feedback** — feedback isn't a critic-family phase even though it looks adjacent. Kimi/cross critic still overrides critique+review only.

**`--depth` flag DOES affect feedback** — same as other author/critic phases, depth rewrites the `:low` suffix to whatever was passed. (Asymmetry principle: feedback is sense-check-style, so probably plateau at `:low`, but honor the flag for the rare case someone wants `:medium`.)

Actually — feedback should probably NOT scale with `--depth` either. It's a calibration phase; if the depth differs across runs, the ratings aren't comparable. Lock at `:low`, ignore `--depth`. Make this explicit in the help text. Use `--phase-model feedback=claude:medium` as the surgical escape hatch if someone really needs it.

### 6. Filter helper update

`megaplan/cli.py:1240` (`_filter_feedback_rows`) currently reads `fb["overall"]["rating"]`. After the schema extension, it should read `effective_rating(fb["overall"])` — falling back to `ai_rating` when `rating` is None. Same for the `--stage` filter at `cli.py:1259`.

### 7. Tests

Revise existing tests in `tests/test_with_feedback.py`:

- Workflow tests (state transitions) — no change.
- Handler test that asserts "no subprocess.run" → revise to "no $EDITOR launched" (the worker call IS a subprocess but it's a model worker, not an interactive editor).

Add new tests in `tests/test_feedback_phase.py`:

- **Schema** — `StageFeedback` with only `ai_rating` set serializes correctly; `effective_rating` falls back appropriately.
- **Template** — `render_template(..., prefilled=fb)` produces a file with `ai_rating:` lines populated and `rating:` lines blank.
- **Parser** — round-trips `ai_*` fields. User-edited `rating:` doesn't clobber existing `ai_rating:`.
- **Prompt builder** — `build_feedback_prompt(plan_dir, state)` produces a prompt that mentions every stage that ran and omits stages that didn't.
- **Handler with mocked worker** — happy path: worker returns valid JSON → feedback.md gets populated `ai_*` fields, state transitions to DONE.
- **Handler with malformed worker output** — parse fails → empty `ai_*` template written, state still transitions to DONE, no exception.
- **Handler with existing user-edited feedback** — `--force` rewrites `ai_*` only, leaves user `rating:` / `comment:` intact.
- **Handler idempotency** — second run without `--force` on a populated feedback.md is a no-op.
- **Filter with AI ratings** — `--min-rating 7` matches a plan with `ai_rating: 8` and no user rating.

## Out of scope

- Don't change the workflow state machine or the `--with-feedback` flag wiring (already shipped).
- Don't add a free-form "what would I do differently" paragraph. The per-stage `comment:` fields are the qualitative output channel.
- Don't make the rating cost-aware. The model sees total cost in the run-meta digest as context but is explicitly told NOT to factor it into ratings.
- Don't auto-push parsed AI feedback to the DB. The existing `_push_feedback_to_db` path fires on interactive `feedback edit` only; that's fine. (Optional extension: push on workflow-mode completion too. Discuss before doing.)
- Don't re-run `feedback` automatically on later plan edits. Once feedback ran for a `done` plan, it's done unless `--force`.

## Invariants

- `feedback.md` schema stays backward-compatible. Old files with only `rating:` / `comment:` still parse correctly and the new `ai_*` fields default to `None`.
- A feedback phase failure (worker error, parse failure, schema violation) NEVER sinks the plan — write an empty `ai_*` template and transition to DONE.
- The mechanical scaffolder behavior is preserved when the worker dispatch fails: user still gets a `feedback.md` they can fill in by hand.
- Existing `feedback edit/show/search` operations stay interactive — only the workflow-mode dispatch changes.

## Acceptance

- `megaplan init <idea> --project-dir . --with-feedback --profile thoughtful` initializes correctly.
- `megaplan auto --plan <name>` runs feedback as a real phase, dispatches a Claude:low worker, parses output, writes a populated `feedback.md` with `ai_rating` / `ai_comment` per stage and Overall.
- `megaplan feedback show --plan <name>` shows the AI ratings.
- User edits `feedback.md`, fills `rating:` on a stage → `megaplan feedback show` reflects the user value (via `effective_rating`).
- `megaplan feedback search --min-rating 7` matches AI-rated runs the user hasn't touched.
- Existing 44 tests in `test_with_feedback.py` still pass (revised where needed).
- All new tests in `test_feedback_phase.py` pass.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Real AI-rated feedback phase

## Overview
The `--with-feedback` flag is already wired through state machine, CLI, init handler, and auto driver. The current `handle_feedback` workflow branch (`megaplan/cli.py:1386-1412`) is a no-op scaffolder that writes an empty `feedback.md` and transitions to DONE. Replace that body with a real worker phase: build per-stage artifact digests, dispatch a `claude:low` worker, parse JSON ratings into new `ai_rating` / `ai_comment` fields on `StageFeedback`, render a populated template, and transition to DONE. Failure must never sink the plan — fall back to the empty-template scaffolder behavior.

Schema extension is backward-compatible (new fields default `None`, `effective_rating` falls back from `rating` to `ai_rating`). Search/filter helpers switch to `effective_rating` so AI-rated runs match `--min-rating` filters before the user touches them.

Profile slots add `feedback = "claude:low"` (or `"claude"` for `all-claude.toml`) across every profile. Feedback is locked to Claude and ignores `--vendor`, `--critic`, and `--depth` for cross-run comparability. `--phase-model feedback=...` is the only escape hatch.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit current feedback module and handler (`megaplan/feedback.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** `megaplan/feedback.py` end-to-end: confirm `StageFeedback`, `PlanFeedback`, `render_template`, `parse_feedback`, `load_feedback`, `_STAGE_BLURBS`, `STAGES` shapes.
2. **Read** `megaplan/cli.py:1240` (`_filter_feedback_rows`), `:1259` (`--stage`), and `:1324-1412` (`handle_feedback` and its `workflow` branch).
3. **Read** `handle_critique` and `handle_review` worker dispatch flows to mirror their pattern (model resolution via profile slot, worker invocation, text→JSON parse).
4. **Read** `megaplan/auto.py::_phase_command` feedback routing and `megaplan/_core/workflow.py::_with_feedback_from_state` to confirm no extra wiring is needed.

### Step 2: Extend schema with AI provenance fields (`megaplan/feedback.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `ai_rating: int | None = None` and `ai_comment: str | None = None` to `StageFeedback` (and to `PlanFeedback.overall` via the same `StageFeedback` shape if it reuses it; otherwise mirror the two fields on the overall dataclass).
2. **Update** `is_empty` to return True only when all four fields are unset.
3. **Update** `to_dict` to emit `rating`, `comment`, `ai_rating`, `ai_comment`.
4. **Extend** `render_template(name, idea, prefilled: PlanFeedback | None = None)` — when `prefilled` is provided, write `ai_rating:` / `ai_comment:` from prefilled data; keep user `rating:` / `comment:` blank.
5. **Extend** `parse_feedback` to recognize `ai_rating:` / `ai_comment:` in each stage section and on overall. Preserve existing behavior for files that only have `rating:` / `comment:`.
6. **Add** `effective_rating(stage_feedback) -> int | None` helper returning `rating if rating is not None else ai_rating`.

### Step 3: Build the feedback prompt module (`megaplan/prompts/feedback.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `megaplan/prompts/feedback.py` exporting `build_feedback_prompt(plan_dir: Path, state: dict) -> str`.
2. **Per-stage digesters** (each 2-5 sentences, all defensive on missing files):
   - prep: read `prep.json` → scope + key findings count.
   - plan: pick latest `plan_v*.md` → summary line + iteration count.
   - critique: latest `critique_v*.json` (fallback `critique_output.json`) → flag count + top categories.
   - revise: diff plan v1→vN summary (file presence + version count is enough; don't run a real diff if expensive).
   - gate: `gate.json` → recommendation + passed.
   - finalize: `final.md` → task/batch counts.
   - execute: `execution.json` + `execution_audit.json` + `execution_batch_*.json` → tasks done/skipped/blocked, batch count, files changed.
   - review: `review.json` → verdict + summary.
3. **Run-meta** block (always): robustness, profile, iteration count, `state["meta"]["total_cost_usd"]`, per-phase durations from history.
4. **Stages-that-didn't-run**: list explicitly as "did not run; do not rate."
5. **Prompt body**: the rubric text from the brief verbatim, then digests, then the strict-JSON response schema. Tell the model to only include stages that actually ran and to keep each comment to one sentence.

### Step 4: Replace `handle_feedback` workflow branch (`megaplan/cli.py:1386-1412`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Verify** `current_state == STATE_REVIEWED` (existing check, keep).
2. **Skip-AI guard**: if `feedback.md` exists, parse it; if any user `rating:`/`comment:` is populated and `--force` is not set → transition to DONE and return without dispatching.
3. **Build prompt** via `build_feedback_prompt(plan_dir, state)`.
4. **Resolve phase model** via the same profile-slot mechanism `handle_critique`/`handle_review` use, with phase name `feedback`. Honor `--phase-model feedback=...` override. Do **not** apply `--vendor`, `--critic`, or `--depth` rewrites to this slot.
5. **Dispatch worker**, capture text response. Wrap in try/except.
6. **Parse JSON** strictly. On parse failure / schema violation: log warning, render empty template (no `prefilled`), atomic write, transition to DONE, set `ai_filled: False`. Never raise.
7. **Happy path**: build `PlanFeedback` from parsed JSON, call `render_template(name, idea, prefilled=parsed)`, atomic write, transition to DONE, set `ai_filled: True`.
8. **`--force` semantics**: when `feedback.md` already exists with user fields, re-parse it, regenerate AI fields, render with the user `rating:`/`comment:` preserved and AI fields overwritten. Add helper to merge if needed.
9. **Return** `StepResponse(feedback_path=..., feedback_present=True, ai_filled=..., state="done")`.

### Step 5: Add `--force` to feedback subparser + update filters (`megaplan/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `--force` to the `feedback` subparser (workflow op only; ignored for `edit`/`show`/`search`).
2. **Update** `_filter_feedback_rows` at `megaplan/cli.py:1240` to use `effective_rating(fb["overall"])`.
3. **Update** the `--stage` filter at `:1259` to use `effective_rating(stage_dict)`.

### Step 6: Add `feedback` slot to all profiles (`megaplan/profiles/*.toml`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `feedback = "claude:low"` to every profile except:
   - `all-claude.toml` → `feedback = "claude"`.
2. **Profiles to touch**: `basic.toml`, `led.toml`, `thoughtful.toml`, `premium.toml` (both variants if separate files), `super-premium.toml`, `poirot.toml`, `standard.toml`, `all-claude.toml`, `all-codex.toml`, `marlowe-*`, `spade-*`, `holmes-*`, `watson-*`, `nancy*`, all-deepseek profiles, `all-open*`. Enumerate via `ls megaplan/profiles/` and add to every TOML file present.
3. **Verify** profile loader recognizes the new slot (most likely no code change needed — it's just a key lookup).

### Step 7: Tests (`tests/test_with_feedback.py`, `tests/test_feedback_phase.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Revise** the existing handler test that asserts "no subprocess.run" → assert "no `$EDITOR` launched" (model worker subprocess is now expected; interactive editor is not).
2. **Create** `tests/test_feedback_phase.py` with:
   - Schema: `StageFeedback` with only `ai_rating` set serializes correctly via `to_dict`.
   - `effective_rating` precedence: user `rating` wins over `ai_rating`; falls back when `rating is None`.
   - Template: `render_template(..., prefilled=fb)` produces file with `ai_rating:` populated, `rating:` blank.
   - Parser round-trip for `ai_*` fields; user-edited `rating:` does not clobber existing `ai_rating:`.
   - `build_feedback_prompt` mentions every stage that ran, omits/marks "did not run" for absent artifacts.
   - Handler happy path (mocked worker → valid JSON → populated `ai_*`, state DONE).
   - Handler malformed worker output → empty `ai_*` template, state DONE, no exception.
   - Handler `--force` rewrites `ai_*` only; preserves user `rating:`/`comment:`.
   - Handler idempotency without `--force` is a no-op when user fields populated.
   - Filter: `--min-rating 7` matches `ai_rating: 8` with no user rating.
3. **Run** `pytest tests/test_with_feedback.py tests/test_feedback_phase.py -x`.
4. **Run** full suite: `pytest tests/ -x` to catch any unintended schema fallout.

## Execution Order
1. Schema extension first (Step 2) — everything else builds on the new fields.
2. Prompt module (Step 3) — pure function, easy to unit-test.
3. Handler replacement + filter update (Steps 4-5) — wires the new pieces.
4. Profile updates (Step 6) — additive TOML edits, low risk.
5. Tests last (Step 7) — both revising existing and adding new.

## Validation Order
1. Run `tests/test_feedback_phase.py` first (cheapest, most targeted).
2. Run `tests/test_with_feedback.py` to confirm revisions pass and the 44 existing tests still work.
3. Run full `pytest tests/` for regression sweep.
4. Manual smoke (info-level): `megaplan init <idea> --project-dir . --with-feedback --profile thoughtful` then `megaplan auto --plan <name>` and inspect `feedback.md`.

    "`PlanFeedback.overall` is shaped like `StageFeedback` (or close enough) so adding `ai_rating`/`ai_comment` covers both per-stage and overall.",
    "Profile loader is permissive about new top-level slots \u2014 adding `feedback = ...` requires no schema/loader change.",
    "Digest size is bounded enough that no truncation is required for typical plans; will add a 2-3KB-per-artifact cap defensively in the prompt builder if size becomes a concern.",
    "`--force` preserves populated user `rating:`/`comment:` fields and only overwrites `ai_*`, per the brief.",
    "The 'workflow op' subparser is the right place to add `--force`; not added to edit/show/search."
  ],
  "structure_warnings": []
}

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic.",

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the real callers for these paths are shell commands that expect actual files. reigh's readme uploads an idea file into `/workspace/megaplan-idea-2week.txt` before calling `megaplan init`, and `chain.sh` immediately checks `-f \"$spec\"` before running `megaplan chain --spec \"$spec\"`. the revised plan now constructs equivalent callers from `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec`, but still never adds the corresponding file-transfer or materialization step, so those callers would receive nonexistent arguments.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the mobile problem is broader than entrypage. `submissionscarouselpage` already has a pre-existing mobile gap because its score panel is desktop-only and its footer actions are also hidden on mobile (`src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347-354` plus `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`). the revised plan says entrypage should 'match the carouselpage pattern', which would spread that existing gap into the one page that currently has working mobile controls.",

      "id": "DEBT-030",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the revised plan correctly narrows the schema-shape problem itself back to the six audited mismatches, but the generated runtime-schema consumer path is broader than the plan acknowledges: `tests/test_parallel_review.py` also reads the runtime `review.json` schema from `schemas_root(repo_root)`, so stale generated schema copies can affect more than the direct schema tests.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-049",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: input provisioning is the broader unresolved concept here. the repo's current railway workflow does not just run commands; it also stages runtime artifacts into `/workspace` first, such as the uploaded idea text in readme line 103 and the expected `/workspace/chain.yaml` consumed by `chain.sh`. the revised plan fixes the runner dispatch itself, but it still treats `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec` as if those files will already exist without describing how cloud deploy makes that true.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-057",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the broader concept is now the validation surface rather than the runtime surface. hermes imports already appear in two non-execution helpers via bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`, so the plan's claim that the helper-plus-gate pattern is self-correcting across future codebase evolution is too broad unless the guard also accounts for bare imports, not just `from ... import ...` forms.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "vendor-hermes-agent-into-the-20260422-0112"

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-060",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: while the docstring on apply_profile_expansion correctly documents the recovery side, the root cause in megaplan/auto.py (_phase_command omitting --profile) has no comment explaining why the flag is intentionally dropped. a future maintainer might add --profile to the subprocess command without realizing it would make the state fallback redundant. however, adding --profile would be harmless because apply_profile_expansion would simply use args.profile directly, so no functional breakage would occur.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-a-short-docstring-comment-20260424-0104"

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-062",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: execute receipt identity: the plan introduces a new collision for multi-batch `--batch` runs. it says execute emits one receipt per handler invocation and `attempt` stays 1, while step 11 emits non-final and final batch receipts with the same `phase=\"execute\"` and `iteration=state[\"iteration\"]`; the plan-dir filename remains `step_receipt_execute_v<iteration>.json`. running batch 1 and then batch 2 in the same iteration would overwrite the plan-dir receipt and create multiple jsonl rows with the same `(plan_id, phase, iteration, attempt)`, contradicting the brief's one-receipt-per-identity contract.",

      "id": "DEBT-066",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked the broader profile-selection concept against built-in profiles. `standard.toml` uses plain `claude`/`codex` agent specs, while `all-open.toml` uses `hermes:<provider/model>` specs; judge auto-selection needs to compare both agent labels and model ids if it is meant to avoid reusing a profile's executor model.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-075",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: mode aliasing is broader than init and prompt builders: `rg` found mode-specific surfaces in cli status display (`megaplan/cli.py:304-307`), execute timeout auditing (`megaplan/execute/timeout.py:77-84`), review pre-check gating (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:283-286`), schema selection, execute merge, workers, and hermes. the plan's file list mentions some of these categories generically, but it does not call out that every prose-artifact check currently written as `doc|joke` needs a creative/form-aware helper.",

      "id": "DEBT-088",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: search for related code: filestore.root (file.py:212) is the *arnold/epic file backend root* (where epic json files live), not a git working tree. tickets in local-only mode are scoped to a repo (`<cwd-repo>/.megaplan/tickets/`), which is conceptually distinct from where the filestore stores epics. step 7.2 says 'pass repo_root = self._root' \u2014 even after fixing the attribute name to `self.root`, walking that path for `.megaplan/tickets/` would look inside the filestore data directory, not in the user's repo. the filestore hook has no clean way to know which repo the agent was working in. flag-010 remains open and the v4 mitigation (`self._root`) does not actually solve it.",

    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 13,

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-009",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-010",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-029",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "robust-review-v2"
        ]

Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

1	"""External user feedback for completed megaplan runs.
2	
3	A `feedback.md` file lives in each plan directory and is owned by the user:
4	they fill in per-stage ratings (0-10) and optional comments after a run
5	finishes. Megaplan only scaffolds the template and parses it back on load —
6	it never overwrites user edits.
7	
8	Old plans without a feedback file simply have ``Plan.feedback`` set to None;
9	running ``megaplan feedback <plan>`` scaffolds the template on demand.
10	"""
11	
12	from __future__ import annotations
13	
14	import re
15	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
16	from pathlib import Path
17	
18	FEEDBACK_FILENAME = "feedback.md"
19	
20	# Canonical workflow stages in pipeline order. Mirrors the transitions in
21	# megaplan/_core/workflow.py; "overall" is rendered separately at the top.
22	STAGES: tuple[str, ...] = (
23	    "prep",
24	    "plan",
25	    "critique",
26	    "revise",
27	    "gate",
28	    "tiebreaker",
29	    "finalize",
30	    "execute",
31	    "review",
32	)
33	
34	_STAGE_BLURBS: dict[str, str] = {
35	    "prep": "Pre-plan research / scoping",
36	    "plan": "Initial plan generation",
37	    "critique": "Parallel critique passes",
38	    "revise": "Plan revisions in response to critique",
39	    "gate": "Quality gate decision",
40	    "tiebreaker": "Tiebreaker orchestration when gates disagreed",
41	    "finalize": "Final plan consolidation",
42	    "execute": "Implementation by the executor",
43	    "review": "Post-execution review",
44	}
45	
46	
47	@dataclass
48	class StageFeedback:
49	    rating: int | None = None
50	    comment: str | None = None
51	
52	    def is_empty(self) -> bool:
53	        return self.rating is None and not (self.comment and self.comment.strip())
54	
55	
56	@dataclass
57	class PlanFeedback:
58	    overall: StageFeedback = field(default_factory=StageFeedback)
59	    stages: dict[str, StageFeedback] = field(default_factory=dict)
60	
61	    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, dict[str, int | str | None]]:
62	        def _one(sf: StageFeedback) -> dict[str, int | str | None]:
63	            return {"rating": sf.rating, "comment": sf.comment}
64	
65	        return {
66	            "overall": _one(self.overall),
67	            "stages": {name: _one(sf) for name, sf in self.stages.items()},
68	        }
69	
70	    def is_empty(self) -> bool:
71	        return self.overall.is_empty() and all(sf.is_empty() for sf in self.stages.values())
72	
73	
74	def feedback_path(plan_dir: Path) -> Path:
75	    return Path(plan_dir) / FEEDBACK_FILENAME
76	
77	
78	def render_template(plan_name: str, *, idea: str | None = None) -> str:
79	    """Render a fresh feedback.md template with all stage fields blank."""
80	
81	    lines: list[str] = [
82	        f"# Feedback for plan: {plan_name}",
83	        "",
84	        "Fill in any fields you want — leave the rest blank. `rating:` is",
85	        "an integer 0–10 (or blank). `comment:` is free text and may span",
86	        "multiple lines (everything until the next `##` heading is the",
87	        "comment body).",
88	        "",
89	    ]
90	    if idea:
91	        lines.extend([f"> {idea.strip()}", ""])
92	
93	    lines.extend(
94	        [
95	            "## Overall",
96	            "",
97	            "rating:",
98	            "comment:",
99	            "",
100	        ]
101	    )
102	
103	    for stage in STAGES:
104	        blurb = _STAGE_BLURBS.get(stage, "")
105	        heading = f"## {stage}"
106	        if blurb:
107	            heading = f"{heading}  <!-- {blurb} -->"
108	        lines.extend([heading, "", "rating:", "comment:", ""])
109	
110	    return "\n".join(lines).rstrip() + "\n"
111	
112	
113	_HEADING_RE = re.compile(r"^##\s+(\S+)", re.MULTILINE)
114	_RATING_RE = re.compile(r"^rating\s*:\s*(.*?)\s*$", re.IGNORECASE | re.MULTILINE)
115	
116	
117	def _parse_rating(raw: str) -> int | None:
118	    raw = raw.strip()
119	    if not raw:
120	        return None
121	    # Accept "8", "8/10", "8 out of 10"
122	    match = re.match(r"^(-?\d+)", raw)
123	    if not match:
124	        return None
125	    try:
126	        value = int(match.group(1))
127	    except ValueError:
128	        return None
129	    if value < 0 or value > 10:
130	        return None
131	    return value
132	
133	
134	def _parse_section(body: str) -> StageFeedback:
135	    """Parse the body under a single `## <name>` heading."""
136	
137	    rating: int | None = None
138	    rating_match = _RATING_RE.search(body)
139	    rating_end = 0
140	    if rating_match is not None:
141	        rating = _parse_rating(rating_match.group(1))
142	        rating_end = rating_match.end()
143	
144	    # Comment: everything after `comment:` until end of section. If `comment:`
145	    # is missing, treat the section as no comment.
146	    comment: str | None = None
147	    comment_match = re.search(
148	        r"^comment\s*:\s*(.*)$",
149	        body[rating_end:],
150	        re.IGNORECASE | re.MULTILINE,
151	    )
152	    if comment_match is not None:
153	        first_line = comment_match.group(1).strip()
154	        rest_start = rating_end + comment_match.end()
155	        rest = body[rest_start:].strip("\n")
156	        parts: list[str] = []
157	        if first_line:
158	            parts.append(first_line)
159	        if rest:
160	            parts.append(rest)
161	        joined = "\n".join(parts).strip()
162	        comment = joined or None
163	
164	    return StageFeedback(rating=rating, comment=comment)
165	
166	
167	def parse_feedback(text: str) -> PlanFeedback:
168	    """Parse a feedback.md document. Unknown headings are kept under stages."""
169	
170	    fb = PlanFeedback()
171	    # Split on `## <heading>` lines while keeping the body that follows each.
172	    parts = re.split(r"^##\s+(\S+).*$", text, flags=re.MULTILINE)
173	    # parts == [preamble, name1, body1, name2, body2, ...]
174	    for i in range(1, len(parts), 2):
175	        name = parts[i].strip().lower()
176	        body = parts[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(parts) else ""
177	        section = _parse_section(body)
178	        if section.is_empty():
179	            continue
180	        if name == "overall":
181	            fb.overall = section
182	        else:
183	            fb.stages[name] = section
184	    return fb
185	
186	
187	def load_feedback(plan_dir: Path) -> PlanFeedback | None:
188	    """Read and parse feedback.md from a plan directory, if it exists."""
189	
190	    path = feedback_path(plan_dir)
191	    if not path.exists():
192	        return None
193	    try:
194	        text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
195	    except OSError:
196	        return None
197	    fb = parse_feedback(text)
198	    return fb if not fb.is_empty() else PlanFeedback()
199	
200	
201	def format_summary(fb: PlanFeedback) -> str:
202	    """Render a short human-readable summary of parsed feedback."""
203	
204	    lines: list[str] = []
205	
206	    def _fmt(name: str, sf: StageFeedback) -> None:
207	        rating = f"{sf.rating}/10" if sf.rating is not None else "—"
208	        lines.append(f"  {name:<10} {rating}")
209	        if sf.comment:

211	                lines.append(f"             {cline}")
212	
213	    lines.append("Overall:")
214	    _fmt("rating", fb.overall)
215	    if fb.stages:
216	        lines.append("")
217	        lines.append("Stages:")
218	        for stage in STAGES:
219	            if stage in fb.stages:
220	                _fmt(stage, fb.stages[stage])
221	        # Any non-canonical stage names the user added
222	        extras = sorted(k for k in fb.stages if k not in STAGES)
223	        for stage in extras:
224	            _fmt(stage, fb.stages[stage])
225	    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"

# `basic` — tier 1 (basic). The all-cheap rung of the rubric ladder.
#
# DeepSeek V4 Pro handles every author/mechanical phase (plan, prep, revise,
# gate, finalize, execute, loop_*, tiebreakers); Kimi handles the two
# sense-check phases (critique, review). No premium models wired in at all.
#
# Use for discovery, docs, and most routine programming — the rubric's tier-1
# bucket where the patterns are stable and the variables are few. Don't
# over-reach: this tier handles a wider range of "I know what to do, just need
# it written" work than the instinct to bump up to `thoughtful` suggests.
#
# Flag interactions:
#
#   * `--vendor claude|codex` — silent no-op. Tier 1 has no premium slots to
#     flip, so the flag is preserved as a config preference but doesn't
#     change the resolved phase map.
#   * `--critic kimi|cross` — Kimi already critiques+reviews, so `--critic
#     kimi` is a no-op. `--critic cross` would expand to "the other premium"
#     relative to the resolved vendor, but since there's no premium in the
#     profile to begin with this is mostly a footgun — prefer to step up to
#     `led` or `thoughtful` if you want a premium critic.
#   * `--phase-model phase=spec` — always honored; the per-phase escape
#     hatch overrides whatever this profile sets.
#
# See docs/megaplan-rubric.md for the full rubric.
[profiles.basic]
plan                  = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro"
prep                  = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro"
critique              = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/kimi-k2p6"
revise                = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro"
gate                  = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro"
finalize              = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro"
execute               = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro"
loop_plan             = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro"
loop_execute          = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro"
review                = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/kimi-k2p6"
tiebreaker_researcher = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro"
tiebreaker_challenger = "hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/deepseek-v4-pro"
[profiles.standard]
plan = "claude"
prep = "claude"

312	def _phase_command(next_step: str) -> list[str]:
313	    """Translate a `next_step` from status into the CLI args that run it.
314	
315	    Most phases are one-to-one: next_step == command. Execute adds the
316	    destructive + user-approved flags because auto-mode implies both.
317	
318	    Multi-token values like ``"override add-note"`` must be split into
319	    ``["override", "add-note"]`` so argparse sees the sub-subcommand —
320	    otherwise the whole string is passed as a single positional and
321	    argparse rejects it with `invalid choice`.
322	    """
323	    if next_step == "execute":
324	        # --retry-blocked-tasks is safe to pass on every iteration. Within a
325	        # single auto session, tasks that report status=blocked terminate the
326	        # auto loop via STATE_AWAITING_HUMAN (see eb4ac447), so re-dispatch
327	        # only happens on a *fresh* `megaplan auto` invocation — which is the
328	        # user's signal that any external prereq has been resolved and stale
329	        # blocked statuses should be retried instead of short-circuiting.
330	        # If there are no blocked tasks, the flag is a no-op.
331	        return [
332	            "execute",
333	            "--confirm-destructive",
334	            "--user-approved",
335	            "--retry-blocked-tasks",
336	        ]
337	    if next_step == "feedback":
338	        # The auto driver must dispatch the *workflow* operation, not the
339	        # default "edit" operation — otherwise the handler would open $EDITOR
340	        # and block on human input.  "feedback workflow" scaffolds the file
341	        # non-interactively and transitions reviewed → done.
342	        return ["feedback", "workflow"]
343	    return shlex.split(next_step)
344	
345

348	    return find_plan_dir(cwd or Path.cwd(), plan)

        Idea:
# Turn the feedback step into a real AI-rated phase

## Goal

The `--with-feedback` flag (already shipped in commit `e7555135`) currently dispatches a mechanical scaffolder that writes an empty `feedback.md` and transitions to DONE. Replace that with a real worker phase: a model (Claude `:low` by default) reads digests of every artifact in the plan dir, rates each workflow stage 0-10 with a comment, and writes a populated `feedback.md`. User can still edit afterward — AI ratings live in parallel `ai_*` fields so user edits are distinguishable from model output.

## Why

`feedback.md` is supposed to be the corpus that flows back into rubric/profile tuning (the `--min-rating` / `--max-rating` search filters in `feedback.py` make this explicit). Empty templates that the user has to manually rate after every run never get filled in. An auto-rated draft that the user reviews/overrides is the difference between "feature shipped" and "useful data corpus."

## What's already in place (don't reinvent)

- **State machine** — `STATE_EXECUTED → review → STATE_REVIEWED → feedback → STATE_DONE`. Done.
- **`--with-feedback` flag wiring** — `megaplan/cli.py` (init parser), `megaplan/handlers/init.py` (persistence), `megaplan/_core/workflow.py` (`_with_feedback_from_state`, workflow patching). Done.
- **Existing handler dispatch** — `handle_feedback` at `megaplan/cli.py:1324` has a `workflow` operation branch (`cli.py:1386-1412`) that's currently the no-op scaffolder. Replace its body.
- **Auto driver** — `megaplan/auto.py` already routes `feedback` as a phase via `_phase_command`. Done.
- **Schema** — `megaplan/feedback.py`: `STAGES`, `_STAGE_BLURBS`, `StageFeedback(rating, comment)`, `PlanFeedback(overall, stages)`, `render_template`, `parse_feedback`, `load_feedback`. Extend, don't replace.
- **44 existing tests** in `tests/test_with_feedback.py`. Most stay valid; the workflow-mode handler test that asserts "no subprocess.run" gets revised (now it DOES call a worker — but never opens `$EDITOR`).

## What to build

### 1. Extend `feedback.py` schema with AI provenance fields

`megaplan/feedback.py`:

- Add `ai_rating: int | None` and `ai_comment: str | None` fields to `StageFeedback`. Default both `None`.
- `is_empty` should still return True when ALL four fields are unset.
- `to_dict` emits all four (`rating`, `comment`, `ai_rating`, `ai_comment`).
- Extend `render_template` to accept an optional `prefilled: PlanFeedback | None` arg. When provided, write `ai_rating:` / `ai_comment:` for each stage from the prefilled data. The user-editable `rating:` / `comment:` lines stay blank.
- Extend `parse_feedback` to parse both pairs of fields. Each section now recognizes `ai_rating:` / `ai_comment:` AND `rating:` / `comment:`.
- Add `effective_rating(stage_feedback) -> int | None` helper: returns `rating` if set, else `ai_rating`. Used by search filters so `--min-rating 7` matches AI-rated runs the user hasn't touched yet.
- Update `_filter_feedback_rows` in `cli.py:1240` to use `effective_rating` instead of hardcoded `rating` lookup.

### 2. New prompt module — `megaplan/prompts/feedback.py`

Build the prompt from per-stage artifact digests. The handler passes in the plan_dir + state; the prompt module reads what's there and assembles digests.

Structure:

```
build_feedback_prompt(plan_dir, state) -> str
```

Each digest is 2-5 sentences summarizing what the phase produced:
- **prep** (if `prep.json` exists): research scope + key findings
- **plan** (`plan_v*.md`): final plan summary, iteration count
- **critique** (`critique_output.json` / `critique_v*.json`): flag count, top issue categories
- **revise** (plan diffs across versions): what changed v1→vN
- **gate** (`gate.json`): recommendation + passed
- **finalize** (`final.md`): task count, batches
- **execute** (`execution.json`, `execution_audit.json`, `execution_batch_*.json`): tasks done/skipped/blocked, batch count, files changed
- **review** (`review.json`): verdict, summary
- **Run meta** (always): robustness, profile, iteration count, total cost from `state["meta"]["total_cost_usd"]`, history durations per phase

Stages that didn't run (because of robustness) are listed as "did not run" and not requested for rating.

The prompt itself:

```
You are a retrospective evaluator. Your job is to rate the quality of each
phase of a completed megaplan run.

This rating feeds into rubric tuning. Be honest. Errors of leniency cost
more than errors of severity — if a phase produced mediocre output that
later phases worked around, mark it ~5, not ~8.

Rate quality only, not cost-effectiveness. A great run that burned $50 is
still a 9 if the output is excellent. A cheap run with sloppy output is
not a 9 because it was cheap.

Scale (0-10):
- 10: textbook; no notes.
- 8: solid; minor polish only.
- 6: workable but with real issues — wasted iterations, missed flags,
     over/under-engineering.
- 4: degraded; the phase didn't do its job, downstream had to compensate.
- 2: actively harmful; produced output that hurt later stages.
- 0: complete failure.

Per-stage rubric:
- prep: did research surface useful info, or was it filler?
- plan: did the plan structure the work appropriately?
- critique: did it catch real issues, or stamp / over-flag?
- revise: did revise actually address critique flags?
- gate: was the decision well-calibrated?
- finalize: did the final plan land cleanly?
- execute: did the executor follow the plan? Add/remove unnecessary scope?
- review: did review catch real issues, or rubber-stamp?

Overall: weighted impression of run quality.

Each comment must be one sentence — what specifically drove the rating.

[digests follow]

Respond with strict JSON only:
{"overall": {"rating": int, "comment": str},
 "stages": {"<stage>": {"rating": int, "comment": str}, ...}}

Only include stages that actually ran.
```

### 3. Handler — `handle_feedback` workflow branch

Replace the body at `megaplan/cli.py:1386-1412`. New flow:

1. Verify `current_state == STATE_REVIEWED`. (Already done; keep.)
2. If `feedback.md` exists AND any user fields (`rating:` / `comment:`) are populated AND `--force` is NOT set: skip the AI pass entirely. Just transition to DONE. (Respects manual override.)
3. Otherwise: build prompt via `build_feedback_prompt(plan_dir, state)`, dispatch through the standard worker path used by other prompt-based phases (critique/review are the references). The phase model resolves through the same profile/slot mechanism — see (5).
4. Parse the model's JSON response. On parse failure or schema violation: log a warning, write a feedback.md with EMPTY `ai_*` fields (so user can still rate manually), transition to DONE. Don't fail the phase — feedback failure must never sink an otherwise-done plan.
5. Render `feedback.md` via the extended `render_template(name, idea=..., prefilled=parsed_feedback)`.
6. Atomic write. Transition state to DONE.
7. Return `StepResponse` with `feedback_path`, `feedback_present: True`, `ai_filled: bool`, `state: "done"`.

`--force` flag: add to the `feedback` subparser. Means "regenerate `ai_*` fields even if `feedback.md` already exists; never touch user `rating:`/`comment:` fields." When --force is set on a `feedback.md` that has user fields, preserve them — only overwrite `ai_*`.

### 4. Worker dispatch

Look at how `handle_critique` or `handle_review` dispatch their worker calls. Same pattern: resolve phase model from profile, build worker, call, get text back, parse. The new phase name is `feedback`. Use the existing infrastructure — `megaplan/workers.py` and friends.

### 5. Profile slots

Every profile in `megaplan/profiles/` gets a `feedback` key. Default model: Claude at `:low` across every tier. Exact specs:

- `basic.toml`: `feedback = "claude:low"`
- `led.toml`: `feedback = "claude:low"`
- `thoughtful.toml`: `feedback = "claude:low"`
- `premium.toml` (claude variant): `feedback = "claude:low"`
- `premium.toml` (codex variant): `feedback = "claude:low"` (intentional — feedback is fixed-vendor for cross-run comparability)
- `super-premium.toml` / `poirot.toml` / `standard.toml` (vendor-locked): `feedback = "claude:low"`
- `all-claude.toml`: `feedback = "claude"` (matches the no-suffix convention)
- `all-codex.toml`: `feedback = "claude:low"` (cross-vendor — feedback is independent of run vendor)
- Detective-cluster legacy profiles (`marlowe-*`, `spade-*`, `holmes-*`, `watson-*`, `nancy`): `feedback = "claude:low"`
- All-deepseek profiles, `all-open`: `feedback = "claude:low"` (only premium phase in an otherwise-open run; the cost is acceptable because feedback runs once per `--with-feedback` plan)

**`--vendor` flag does NOT affect feedback** — feedback stays Claude regardless of which premium vendor is doing plan/critique/etc. This is the cross-comparability principle: a `thoughtful @claude` run and a `thoughtful @codex` run should be rated by the same evaluator.

**`--critic` flag does NOT extend to feedback** — feedback isn't a critic-family phase even though it looks adjacent. Kimi/cross critic still overrides critique+review only.

**`--depth` flag DOES affect feedback** — same as other author/critic phases, depth rewrites the `:low` suffix to whatever was passed. (Asymmetry principle: feedback is sense-check-style, so probably plateau at `:low`, but honor the flag for the rare case someone wants `:medium`.)

Actually — feedback should probably NOT scale with `--depth` either. It's a calibration phase; if the depth differs across runs, the ratings aren't comparable. Lock at `:low`, ignore `--depth`. Make this explicit in the help text. Use `--phase-model feedback=claude:medium` as the surgical escape hatch if someone really needs it.

### 6. Filter helper update

`megaplan/cli.py:1240` (`_filter_feedback_rows`) currently reads `fb["overall"]["rating"]`. After the schema extension, it should read `effective_rating(fb["overall"])` — falling back to `ai_rating` when `rating` is None. Same for the `--stage` filter at `cli.py:1259`.

### 7. Tests

Revise existing tests in `tests/test_with_feedback.py`:

- Workflow tests (state transitions) — no change.
- Handler test that asserts "no subprocess.run" → revise to "no $EDITOR launched" (the worker call IS a subprocess but it's a model worker, not an interactive editor).

Add new tests in `tests/test_feedback_phase.py`:

- **Schema** — `StageFeedback` with only `ai_rating` set serializes correctly; `effective_rating` falls back appropriately.
- **Template** — `render_template(..., prefilled=fb)` produces a file with `ai_rating:` lines populated and `rating:` lines blank.
- **Parser** — round-trips `ai_*` fields. User-edited `rating:` doesn't clobber existing `ai_rating:`.
- **Prompt builder** — `build_feedback_prompt(plan_dir, state)` produces a prompt that mentions every stage that ran and omits stages that didn't.
- **Handler with mocked worker** — happy path: worker returns valid JSON → feedback.md gets populated `ai_*` fields, state transitions to DONE.
- **Handler with malformed worker output** — parse fails → empty `ai_*` template written, state still transitions to DONE, no exception.
- **Handler with existing user-edited feedback** — `--force` rewrites `ai_*` only, leaves user `rating:` / `comment:` intact.
- **Handler idempotency** — second run without `--force` on a populated feedback.md is a no-op.
- **Filter with AI ratings** — `--min-rating 7` matches a plan with `ai_rating: 8` and no user rating.

## Out of scope

- Don't change the workflow state machine or the `--with-feedback` flag wiring (already shipped).
- Don't add a free-form "what would I do differently" paragraph. The per-stage `comment:` fields are the qualitative output channel.
- Don't make the rating cost-aware. The model sees total cost in the run-meta digest as context but is explicitly told NOT to factor it into ratings.
- Don't auto-push parsed AI feedback to the DB. The existing `_push_feedback_to_db` path fires on interactive `feedback edit` only; that's fine. (Optional extension: push on workflow-mode completion too. Discuss before doing.)
- Don't re-run `feedback` automatically on later plan edits. Once feedback ran for a `done` plan, it's done unless `--force`.

## Invariants

- `feedback.md` schema stays backward-compatible. Old files with only `rating:` / `comment:` still parse correctly and the new `ai_*` fields default to `None`.
- A feedback phase failure (worker error, parse failure, schema violation) NEVER sinks the plan — write an empty `ai_*` template and transition to DONE.
- The mechanical scaffolder behavior is preserved when the worker dispatch fails: user still gets a `feedback.md` they can fill in by hand.
- Existing `feedback edit/show/search` operations stay interactive — only the workflow-mode dispatch changes.

## Acceptance

- `megaplan init <idea> --project-dir . --with-feedback --profile thoughtful` initializes correctly.
- `megaplan auto --plan <name>` runs feedback as a real phase, dispatches a Claude:low worker, parses output, writes a populated `feedback.md` with `ai_rating` / `ai_comment` per stage and Overall.
- `megaplan feedback show --plan <name>` shows the AI ratings.
- User edits `feedback.md`, fills `rating:` on a stage → `megaplan feedback show` reflects the user value (via `effective_rating`).
- `megaplan feedback search --min-rating 7` matches AI-rated runs the user hasn't touched.
- Existing 44 tests in `test_with_feedback.py` still pass (revised where needed).
- All new tests in `test_feedback_phase.py` pass.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Real AI-rated feedback phase

## Overview
Approach is sound; the critique flags are all location/coverage gaps, not a wrong-root-cause signal. Revision adds the missing touch points: `megaplan/types.py:DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` must learn `feedback` (otherwise every profile TOML fails validation), the `apply_vendor_rewrite`/`apply_depth_rewrite`/`apply_critic_rewrite` functions must skip the `feedback` slot, the `tiebreaker` stage needs explicit handling, `_render_feedback_table` and `format_summary` need `effective_rating`, `_resume_active_states` gets a `feedback` entry (DEBT-092), and parser regex anchoring is pinned down.

The `--with-feedback` flag is already wired (state machine, CLI init, auto driver). The current `handle_feedback` workflow branch (`megaplan/cli.py:1386-1412`) is a no-op scaffolder. Replace its body with a real worker that builds per-stage artifact digests, dispatches `claude:low`, parses JSON, and writes `ai_rating`/`ai_comment` fields. Failures fall back to the empty-template scaffolder so feedback never sinks a plan.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit current feedback module, handler, and routing infra (`megaplan/feedback.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/types.py`, `megaplan/profiles/__init__.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** `megaplan/feedback.py` end-to-end: `STAGES` (note: includes `tiebreaker`), `_STAGE_BLURBS`, `StageFeedback`, `PlanFeedback`, `render_template`, `parse_feedback`, `load_feedback`, `format_summary` (`:201-225`), and `_RATING_RE`/`_COMMENT_RE` regexes.
2. **Read** `megaplan/cli.py`: `_filter_feedback_rows` (`:1240`), `--stage` filter (`:1259`), `_render_feedback_table` (`:1271-1298`), `handle_feedback` and its `workflow` branch (`:1324`, `:1386-1412`).
3. **Read** `megaplan/types.py:319-332` (`DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING`) and `megaplan/profiles/__init__.py:104-114` (validator + `apply_vendor_rewrite` / `apply_depth_rewrite` / `apply_critic_rewrite`).
4. **Read** `megaplan/workers.py:2381` (fallback indexes `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING[step]`) and `handle_critique`/`handle_review` to mirror their worker dispatch.
5. **Read** `megaplan/auto.py::_phase_command` feedback routing and `megaplan/_core/workflow.py::_with_feedback_from_state` plus `_resume_active_states` (DEBT-092 entry point).

### Step 2: Extend feedback schema with AI provenance (`megaplan/feedback.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `ai_rating: int | None = None` and `ai_comment: str | None = None` to `StageFeedback` (and mirror on the overall dataclass if it isn't already a `StageFeedback`).
2. **Update** `is_empty` to require all four fields unset.
3. **Update** `to_dict` to emit `rating`, `comment`, `ai_rating`, `ai_comment`.
4. **Extend** `render_template(name, idea, prefilled: PlanFeedback | None = None)`. With `prefilled`, write populated `ai_rating:` / `ai_comment:` lines and blank `rating:` / `comment:` lines for each stage in `STAGES` (including `tiebreaker`) and the overall block.
5. **Extend** `parse_feedback` to recognize `ai_rating:` / `ai_comment:`. **Regex anchoring contract**: add `_AI_RATING_RE = r'^ai_rating\s*:\s*(.*?)\s*$'` / `_AI_COMMENT_RE = r'^ai_comment\s*:\s*(.*?)\s*$'`, both line-anchored. Match `_AI_RATING_RE` / `_AI_COMMENT_RE` **before** the existing `_RATING_RE` / `_COMMENT_RE` in each line scan, OR tighten existing regexes to `^rating\s*:` (no `ai_` prefix lookbehind needed since `ai_rating:` won't match `^rating:`; verify with a unit test). Document the ordering in a one-line comment.
6. **Add** `effective_rating(stage_feedback) -> int | None`: returns `rating if rating is not None else ai_rating`. Add `effective_comment(stage_feedback) -> str | None` with the same fallback for display paths.
7. **Update** `format_summary` (`feedback.py:201-225`) to use `effective_rating` / `effective_comment` so `megaplan feedback show` displays AI ratings when user fields are unset. Indicate AI provenance in the rendered string (e.g., `8 (AI)` vs `8`) so the user can tell at a glance.

### Step 3: Routing & profile-loader integration (`megaplan/types.py`, `megaplan/profiles/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `"feedback"` to `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` in `megaplan/types.py:319-332` with value `"claude:low"`. This is what unlocks `VALID_PHASE_KEYS` and makes the `workers.py:2381` fallback safe.
2. **Skip-feedback contract** in `megaplan/profiles/__init__.py`: in `apply_vendor_rewrite`, `apply_depth_rewrite`, and `apply_critic_rewrite`, explicitly bypass the `feedback` slot so the brief's lock-at-`:low` invariant holds. Add a single shared constant `_FEEDBACK_FIXED_PHASES = frozenset({"feedback"})` (or equivalent) and gate each rewrite function on it.
3. **Help text**: add a note to the relevant flag help strings in `cli.py` that `--vendor`/`--depth`/`--critic` do not affect the `feedback` slot; use `--phase-model feedback=...` to override.

### Step 4: Add `feedback` slot to all profiles (`megaplan/profiles/*.toml`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Enumerate** `megaplan/profiles/*.toml`. **Add** `feedback = "claude:low"` to every file, except `all-claude.toml` which gets `feedback = "claude"` (no-suffix convention).
2. With Step 3 landed, profile validation will accept the new slot.

### Step 5: Build the feedback prompt module (`megaplan/prompts/feedback.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `megaplan/prompts/feedback.py` exporting `build_feedback_prompt(plan_dir: Path, state: dict) -> str`.
2. **Per-stage digesters** (each 2-5 sentences, defensive on missing files). Cover every entry in `feedback.py:STAGES`, including `tiebreaker`:
   - prep: `prep.json` → scope + key findings count.
   - plan: latest `plan_v*.md` → summary line + iteration count.
   - critique: latest `critique_v*.json` (fallback `critique_output.json`) → flag count + top categories.
   - revise: plan version count, brief summary of v1→vN deltas (file presence + version count is sufficient).
   - gate: `gate.json` → recommendation + passed.
   - tiebreaker: `tiebreaker*.json` or equivalent artifact → which path was chosen and why (omit/list-as-"did not run" if absent in standard robustness).
   - finalize: `final.md` → task/batch counts.
   - execute: `execution.json` + `execution_audit.json` + `execution_batch_*.json` → tasks done/skipped/blocked, batch count, files changed.
   - review: `review.json` → verdict + summary.
3. **Run-meta** block: robustness, profile, iteration count, `state["meta"]["total_cost_usd"]`, per-phase durations.
4. **Stages-that-didn't-run**: emit "did not run; do not rate" for each absent stage. Tell the model to omit those keys in the response.
5. **Prompt body**: rubric text from the brief verbatim **plus a `tiebreaker` rubric line**: "tiebreaker: did the decision pick the better branch on the available evidence?" Then digests, then the strict-JSON response schema.

### Step 6: Replace `handle_feedback` workflow branch + filters + display (`megaplan/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Verify** `current_state == STATE_REVIEWED` (existing check, keep).
2. **Skip-AI guard**: if `feedback.md` exists, parse it; if any user `rating:`/`comment:` populated and `--force` not set → transition to DONE and return without dispatching.
3. **Build prompt** via `build_feedback_prompt(plan_dir, state)`.
4. **Resolve phase model** via the same profile-slot mechanism `handle_critique`/`handle_review` use, with phase name `feedback`. Honor `--phase-model feedback=...`. Rewrite skips from Step 3 ensure `--vendor`/`--depth`/`--critic` don't touch this slot.
5. **Dispatch worker**, capture text. Wrap in try/except.
6. **Parse JSON** strictly. On parse failure / schema violation / worker error: log warning, render empty template (no `prefilled`), atomic write, transition to DONE, `ai_filled=False`. Never raise.
7. **Happy path**: build `PlanFeedback` from JSON (only stages present in response; others remain `None`), call `render_template(name, idea, prefilled=parsed)`. **`--force` merge**: if existing `feedback.md` has user `rating:`/`comment:`, re-parse it and pass a merged `PlanFeedback` to `render_template` so user fields are preserved while `ai_*` are overwritten. Atomic write. Transition to DONE.
8. **Return** `StepResponse(feedback_path=..., feedback_present=True, ai_filled=..., state="done")`.
9. **Add `--force`** to the `feedback` subparser (workflow op only).
10. **Update** `_filter_feedback_rows` (`:1240`) and `--stage` filter (`:1259`) to use `effective_rating`.
11. **Update** `_render_feedback_table` (`:1271-1298`) to use `effective_rating` for the OVR column and `effective_comment` for the first-line preview; mark AI-only rows (e.g., suffix `(AI)` or a dedicated column flag).

### Step 7: Hygiene — `_resume_active_states` feedback entry (DEBT-092) (`megaplan/_core/workflow.py` or wherever `_resume_active_states` lives)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** the missing `feedback` entry to `_resume_active_states` so resume-from-crash recognizes the phase. Mirror the shape used for `review`.

### Step 8: Tests (`tests/test_with_feedback.py`, `tests/test_feedback_phase.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Revise** the existing `test_with_feedback.py` handler test that asserts "no subprocess.run" → assert "no `$EDITOR` launched" (model worker subprocess is now expected).
2. **Create** `tests/test_feedback_phase.py` covering:
   - Schema: `StageFeedback` with only `ai_rating` serializes via `to_dict`; `effective_rating` precedence; `is_empty` requires all four fields unset.
   - Template: `render_template(..., prefilled=fb)` populates `ai_rating:` / `ai_comment:`, leaves `rating:` / `comment:` blank, covers all `STAGES` including `tiebreaker`.
   - Parser: round-trips `ai_*`; user-edited `rating:` doesn't clobber existing `ai_rating:`; **regression test** ensuring `ai_rating: 8` does not also populate `rating` (regex non-overlap).
   - Prompt builder: mentions every stage that has artifacts (including `tiebreaker`), marks absent stages "did not run".
   - Routing: loading any TOML profile with `feedback = "claude:low"` succeeds; `apply_vendor_rewrite('codex')` / `apply_depth_rewrite('medium')` / `apply_critic_rewrite('kimi')` leave the `feedback` slot at `claude:low`.
   - Handler happy path (mocked worker → valid JSON → populated `ai_*`, state DONE).
   - Handler malformed worker output → empty template, state DONE, no exception.
   - Handler `--force` merge: existing user `rating:`/`comment:` preserved, `ai_*` overwritten.
   - **Idempotency** (callers flag): note that after the first run, state is DONE so the STATE_REVIEWED guard short-circuits; seed state back to `STATE_REVIEWED` with a populated `feedback.md` for the no-op-without-`--force` test. Document this in a test comment.
   - Display: `_render_feedback_table` shows AI ratings when only `ai_*` set; `format_summary` likewise.
   - Filter: `--min-rating 7` matches `ai_rating: 8` with no user rating.
3. **Run** `pytest tests/test_with_feedback.py tests/test_feedback_phase.py -x`.
4. **Run** full suite: `pytest tests/ -x`.

## Execution Order
1. Routing infra first (Step 3) — without `DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING` change, every other piece touching profiles fails.
2. Schema extension (Step 2).
3. Profile TOML edits (Step 4).
4. Prompt module (Step 5).
5. Handler + filters + display (Step 6).
6. Resume-state hygiene (Step 7).
7. Tests (Step 8).

## Validation Order
1. Profile loader sanity: load every profile in `megaplan/profiles/` programmatically (small smoke test) to confirm Step 3 + 4 land cleanly before deeper logic.
2. Run `tests/test_feedback_phase.py` (cheapest, targeted).
3. Run `tests/test_with_feedback.py` to confirm revisions and the 44 existing tests pass.
4. Full `pytest tests/ -x` regression sweep.
5. Manual smoke (info): `megaplan init <idea> --project-dir . --with-feedback --profile thoughtful` then `megaplan auto --plan <name>`; inspect `feedback.md` and run `megaplan feedback show --plan <name>` / `megaplan feedback search --min-rating 7`.
